This map contains both keys that are cached from read operations as well as ‘dirty’ keys which map to a value that is potentially different than what is in the underlying `KVStore`.\n./store/cachekv/README.md:51:Similar to `deleted`, this is a set of keys that are dirty and will need to be updated in the parent `KVStore` upon a write. Keys are mapped to an empty struct to implement a set.\n./store/cachekv/README.md:79:A value being deleted from the `KVStore` is represented with a `nil` value in `cache`, and an insertion of the key into the `deleted` set. `Delete` does not write to `parent`. \n./store/cachekv/README.md:89:If a key is marked for deletion (checked with `isDeleted()`), then `parent.Delete()` is called. [CommitKVStoreCache](#commitkvstorecache)\n./docs/spec/store/interblock-cache.md:25:The inter-block cache is an in-memory cache storing (in-most-cases) immutable state that modules need to read in between blocks. When enabled, all sub-stores of a multi store, e.g., `rootmulti`, are wrapped.\n./docs/spec/store/interblock-cache.md:31:The goal of the inter-block cache is to allow SDK modules to have fast access to data that it is typically queried during the execution of every block. This is data that do not change often, e.g. module parameters. The inter-block cache wraps each `CommitKVStore` of a multi store such as `rootmulti` with a fixed size, write-through cache. Caches are not cleared after a block is committed, as opposed to other caching layers such as `cachekv`.\n./docs/spec/store/interblock-cache.md:36:* `KVCache` is a `CommitKVStore` wrapped with a cache.\n./docs/spec/store/interblock-cache.md:54:The specification also assumes that `CommitKVStore` offers the following API:\n./docs/spec/store/interblock-cache.md:56:* `Get(key string)` attempts to retrieve a key/value pair from `CommitKVStore`.\n./docs/spec/store/interblock-cache.md:57:* `Set(key, string, value []byte)` inserts a key/value pair into the `CommitKVStore`.\n./docs/spec/store/interblock-cache.md:58:* `Delete(key string)` removes the key/value pair identified by `key` from `CommitKVStore`.\n./docs/spec/store/interblock-cache.md:60:> Ideally, both `Cache` and `CommitKVStore` should be specified in a different document and referenced here.\n./docs/spec/store/interblock-cache.md:73:The inter-block cache transparently delegates `Commit()` to its aggregate `CommitKVStore`. If the \n./docs/spec/store/interblock-cache.md:74:aggregate `CommitKVStore` supports atomic writes and use them to guarantee that the store is always in a consistent state in disk, the inter-block cache can be transparently moved to a consistent state when a failure occurs.\n./docs/spec/store/interblock-cache.md:76:> Note that this is the case for `IAVLStore`, the preferred `CommitKVStore`. On commit, it calls `SaveVersion()` on the underlying `MutableTree`. `SaveVersion` writes to disk are atomic via batching. This means that only consistent versions of the store (the tree) are written to the disk. Thus, in case of a failure during a `SaveVersion` call, on recovery from disk, the version of the store will be consistent.\n./docs/spec/store/interblock-cache.md:80:Iteration over each wrapped store is supported via the embedded `CommitKVStore` interface.\n./docs/spec/store/interblock-cache.md:88:`CommitKVCacheManager` implements the cache manager. IAVL is meant to be a standalone Merkelized key/value database, however it's using a KV DB engine to store all tree nodes. So, each node is stored in a separate record in the KV DB. This causes many inefficiencies and problems:\n./docs/architecture/adr-040-storage-and-smt-state-commitments.md:25:* Updates in IAVL may trigger tree reorganization and possible O(log(n)) hashes re-computation, which can become a CPU bottleneck.\n./docs/architecture/adr-040-storage-and-smt-state-commitments.md:26:* The node structure is pretty expensive - it contains a standard tree node elements (key, value, left and right element) and additional metadata such as height, version (which is not required by the Cosmos SDK). The entire node is hashed, and that hash is used as the key in the underlying database, [ref](https://github.com/cosmos/iavl/blob/master/docs/node/node.md\n./docs/architecture/adr-040-storage-and-smt-state-commitments.md:29:Moreover, the IAVL project lacks support and a maintainer and we already see better and well-established alternatives. Instead of optimizing the IAVL, we are looking into other solutions for both storage and state commitments.\n./docs/architecture/adr-040-storage-and-smt-state-commitments.md:33:We propose to separate the concerns of state commitment (**SC**), needed for consensus, and state storage (**SS**), needed for state machine. Finally we replace IAVL with [Celestia's SMT](https://github.com/lazyledger/smt). Celestia SMT is based on Diem (called jellyfish) design [*] - it uses a compute-optimised SMT by replacing subtrees with only default values with a single node (same approach is used by Ethereum2) and implements compact proofs.\n./docs/architecture/adr-040-storage-and-smt-state-commitments.md:47:1. The multistore allows developers to declare any number of [`KVStores`](../advanced/04-store.md#base-layer-kvstores). These `KVStores` only accept the `[]byte` type as value and therefore any custom structure needs to be marshalled using [a codec](../advanced/05-encoding.md) before being stored.\n./docs/docs/learn/intro/03-sdk-design.md:87:Each module can be seen as a little state-machine. Developers need to define the subset of the state handled by the module, as well as custom message types that modify the state (*Note:* `messages` are extracted from `transactions` by `baseapp`). In general, each module declares its own `KVStore` in the `multistore` to persist the subset of the state it defines. Most developers will need to access other 3rd party modules when building their own modules. Given that the Cosmos SDK is an open framework, some of the modules may be malicious, which means there is a need for security principles to reason about inter-module interactions. These principles are based on [object-capabilities](../advanced/10-ocap.md). In practice, this means that instead of having each module keep an access control list for other modules, each module implements special objects called `keepers` that can be passed to other modules to grant a pre-defined set of capabilities.\n./docs/docs/learn/intro/01-why-app-specific.md:54:* Developers are free to explore the full spectrum of tradeoffs (e.g. number of validators vs transaction The multistore is a store of `KVStores` that follows the `Multistore` interface:\n./docs/docs/learn/advanced/04-store.md:102:https://github.com/cosmos/cosmos-sdk/blob/v0.50.0-alpha.0/store/types/store.go#L123-L155\n./docs/docs/learn/advanced/04-store.md:105:If tracing is enabled, then branching the multistore will firstly wrap all the underlying `KVStore` in [`TraceKv.Store`](#tracekv-store).\n./docs/docs/learn/advanced/04-store.md:107:### CommitMultiStore\n./docs/docs/learn/advanced/04-store.md:109:The main type of `Multistore` used in the Cosmos SDK is `CommitMultiStore`, which is an extension of the `Multistore` interface:\n./docs/docs/learn/advanced/04-store.md:112:https://github.com/cosmos/cosmos-sdk/blob/v0.50.0-alpha.0/store/types/store.go#L164-L227\n./docs/docs/learn/advanced/04-store.md:115:As for concrete implementation, the [`rootMulti.Store`] is the go-to implementation of the `CommitMultiStore` interface.\n./docs/docs/learn/advanced/04-store.md:118:https://github.com/cosmos/cosmos-sdk/blob/v0.50.0-alpha.0/store/rootmulti/store.go#L53-L77\n./docs/docs/learn/advanced/04-store.md:121:The `rootMulti.Store` is a base-layer multistore built around a `db` on top of which multiple `KVStores` can be mounted, and is the default multistore store used in [`baseapp`](./00-baseapp.md).\n./docs/docs/learn/advanced/04-store.md:125:Whenever the `rootMulti.Store` needs to be branched, a [`cachemulti.Store`](https://github.com/cosmos/cosmos-sdk/blob/v0.50.0-alpha.0/store/cachemulti/store.go) is used.\n./docs/docs/learn/advanced/04-store.md:128:https://github.com/cosmos/cosmos-sdk/blob/v0.50.0-alpha.0/store/cachemulti/store.go#L19-L33\n./docs/docs/learn/advanced/04-store.md:131:`cachemulti.Store` branches all substores (creates a virtual store for each substore) in its constructor and hold them in `Store.stores`. Moreover caches all read queries. `Store.GetKVStore()` returns the store from `Store.stores`, and `Store.Write()` recursively calls `CacheWrap.Write()` on all the substores.\n./docs/docs/learn/advanced/04-store.md:133:## Base-layer KVStores\n./docs/docs/learn/advanced/04-store.md:135:### `KVStore` and `CommitKVStore` Interfaces\n./docs/docs/learn/advanced/04-store.md:137:A `KVStore` is a simple key-value store used to store and retrieve data. A `CommitKVStore` is a `KVStore` that also implements a `Committer`. By default, stores mounted in `baseapp`'s main `CommitMultiStore` are `CommitKVStore`s. The `KVStore` interface is primarily used to restrict modules from accessing the committer.\n./docs/docs/learn/advanced/04-store.md:139:Individual `KVStore`s are used by modules to manage a subset of the global state. `KVStores` can be accessed by objects that hold a specific key. This `key` should only be exposed to the [`keeper`](../../build/building-modules/06-keeper.md) of the module that defines the store.\n./docs/docs/learn/advanced/04-store.md:141:`CommitKVStore`s are declared by proxy of their respective `key` and mounted on the application's [multistore](#multistore) in the [main application file](../beginner/00-app-anatomy.md#core-application-file). In the same file, the `key` is also passed to the module's `keeper` that is responsible for managing the store.\n./docs/docs/learn/advanced/04-store.md:144:https://github.com/cosmos/cosmos-sdk/blob/v0.50.0-alpha.0/store/types/store.go#L229-L266\n./docs/docs/learn/advanced/04-store.md:147:Apart from the traditional `Get` and `Set` methods, that a `KVStore` must implement via the `BasicKVStore` interface; a `KVStore` must provide an `Iterator(start, end)` method which returns an `Iterator` object. This store is primarily used within [Transient Stores](#transient-store)\n./docs/docs/learn/advanced/04-store.md:181:`Transient.Store` is a base-layer `KVStore` which is automatically discarded at the end of the block.\n./docs/docs/learn/advanced/04-store.md:184:https://github.com/cosmos/cosmos-sdk/blob/v0.50.0-alpha.0/store/transient/store.go#L16-L19\n./docs/docs/learn/advanced/04-store.md:187:`Transient.Store` is a `dbadapter.Store` with a `dbm.NewMemDB()`.
